Filters may refer to fields generated by\n * a transform.\n *\n * See {@link Transform} for details on how each transform works.\n */\npublic class TransformSpec\n{\n  public static final TransformSpec NONE = new TransformSpec(null, null);\n\n  private final DimFilter filter;\n  private final List<Transform> transforms;\n\n  @JsonCreator\n  public TransformSpec(\n      @JsonProperty(\"filter\") final DimFilter filter,\n      @JsonProperty(\"transforms\") final List<Transform> transforms\n  )\n  {\n    this.filter = filter;\n    this.transforms = transforms == null ? ImmutableList.of() : transforms;\n\n    // Check for name collisions.\n    final Set<String> seen = new HashSet<>();\n    for (Transform transform : this.transforms) {\n      if (!seen.add(transform.getName())) {\n        throw new ISE(\"Transform name '%s' cannot be used twice\", transform.getName());\n      }\n    }\n  }\n\n  public static <T> TransformSpec fromInputRowParser(final InputRowParser<T> parser)\n  {\n    // Hack: some firehoses and input specs must extract transformSpec from the parser, since they do not\n    // actually use the parser, but still must respect the transformSpec. This method should extract whatever\n    // transformSpec \"decorate\" had put in.\n\n    if (parser instanceof TransformingInputRowParser) {\n      return ((TransformingInputRowParser) parser).getTransformSpec();\n    } else if (parser instanceof TransformingStringInputRowParser) {\n      return ((TransformingStringInputRowParser) parser).getTransformSpec();\n    } else {\n      throw new ISE(\"Parser was not decorated, but should have been\");\n    }\n  }\n\n  @JsonProperty\n  @Nullable\n  public DimFilter getFilter()\n  {\n    return filter;\n  }\n\n  @JsonProperty\n  public List<Transform> getTransforms()\n  {\n    return transforms;\n  }\n\n  public <T> InputRowParser<T> decorate(final InputRowParser<T> parser)\n  {\n    // Always decorates, even if the transformSpec is a no-op. This is so fromInputRowParser can insist that the\n    // parser is a transforming parser, and possibly help detect coding errors where someone forgot to call \"decorate\".\n\n    if (parser instanceof StringInputRowParser) {\n      // Hack to support the fact that some callers use special methods in StringInputRowParser, such as\n      // parse(String) and startFileFromBeginning.\n      return (InputRowParser<T>) new TransformingStringInputRowParser(\n          parser.getParseSpec(),\n          ((StringInputRowParser) parser).getEncoding(),\n          this\n      );\n    } else {\n      return new TransformingInputRowParser<>(parser, this);\n    }\n  }\n\n  public InputSourceReader decorate(InputSourceReader reader)\n  {\n    return new TransformingInputSourceReader(reader, toTransformer());\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Create a {@link Transformer} from this TransformSpec, when the rows to be transformed do not have a known\n   * signature.\n   */\n  public Transformer toTransformer()\n  {\n    return new Transformer(this);\n  }\n\n  @Override\n  public boolean equals(final Object o)\n  {\n    if (this == o) {\n      return true;\n    }\n    if (o == null || getClass() != o.getClass()) {\n      return false;\n    }\n    final TransformSpec that = (TransformSpec) o;\n    return Objects.equals(filter, that.filter) &&\n           Objects.equals(transforms, that.transforms);\n  }\n\n  @Override\n  public int hashCode()\n  {\n    return Objects.hash(filter, transforms);\n  }\n\n  @Override\n  public String toString()\n  {\n    return \"TransformSpec{\" +\n           \"filter=\" + filter +\n           \", transforms=\" + transforms +\n           '}';\n  }\n}\n","numLines":158,"startLine":1,"totalLines":158}}}

The issue is that:\n\n1. The `/druid/indexer/v1/sampler` endpoint accepts a SamplerSpec (like IndexTaskSamplerSpec)\n2. This spec has a DataSchema which contains a TransformSpec\n3. The TransformSpec has a DimFilter which can be of type \"javascript\"\n4. The JavaScriptDimFilter allows execution of arbitrary JavaScript code\n5. The empty key `\"\"` in the exploit bypasses JavaScript security settings\n\nThe problem is that when the filter is processed, it allows access to Java classes like `java.lang.Runtime` which can execute system commands.\n\nLooking at the code:\n- `JavaScriptDimFilter.java` line 162 checks if JavaScript is enabled: `Preconditions.checkState(config.isEnabled(), \"JavaScript is disabled\");`\n- But when it's enabled, it doesn't restrict what JavaScript can do\n- The JavaScript execution happens in `JavaScriptPredicateFactory` which compiles and executes the script without proper sandboxing\n\nThe fix should be to:\n1. Add validation to prevent dangerous JavaScript patterns like `java.lang.Runtime`, `java.io.File`, etc.\n2. Or disable JavaScript filter support entirely\n3. Or add proper sandboxing\n\nLet me check what tests exist for this vulnerability to understand what legitimate behavior should be preserved.","signature":"b93595768a62c803dc81cf0e91629f11f08535c36100e30921a707047f0190e3"}],"model":"MiniMax-M2","stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2050,"output_tokens":0,"cache_creation_input_tokens":195,"cache_read_input_tokens":33824},"service_tier":"standard","context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"099f0b81-3c05-41e5-90f6-df62d796d109","uuid":"bf809039-1ffe-4306-9cec-cf79f5309e90"}

       }\n\n        final int size = selectorList.length;\n        final Object[] args = new Object[size + 1];\n\n        args[0] = current;\n        for (int i = 0; i < size; i++) {\n          final BaseObjectColumnValueSelector selector = selectorList[i];\n          if (selector != null) {\n            final Object arg = selector.getObject();\n            if (arg != null && arg.getClass().isArray()) {\n              // Context.javaToJS on an array sort of works, although it returns false for Array.isArray(...) and\n              // may have other issues too. Let's just copy the array and wrap that.\n              args[i + 1] = cx.newArray(scope, arrayToObjectArray(arg));\n            } else if (arg instanceof List) {\n              // Using toArray(Object[]), instead of just toArray(), because Arrays.asList()'s impl and similar List\n              // impls could clone the underlying array in toArray(), that could be not Object[], but e. g.
